=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Alice is attempting to type a specific string on her computer. However, she tends to be clumsy and may press a key for too long, resulting in a character being typed multiple times.
Although Alice tried to focus on her typing, she is aware that she may still have done this at most once.
You are given a string word, which represents the final output displayed on Alice's screen.
Return the total number of possible original strings that Alice might have intended to type.
 
Example 1:

Input: word = "abbcccc"
Output: 5
Explanation:
The possible strings are: "abbcccc", "abbccc", "abbcc", "abbc", and "abcccc".

Example 2:

Input: word = "abcd"
Output: 1
Explanation:
The only possible string is "abcd".

Example 3:

Input: word = "aaaa"
Output: 4

 
Constraints:

1 <= word.length <= 100
word consists only of lowercase English letters.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def possibleStringCount(self, word: str) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

```
class Solution:
    def possibleStringCount(self, word: str) -> int:
        ans = 1
        for i in range(1, len(word)):
            if word[i] == word[i-1]:
                ans += 1
        return ans
```

```
class Solution:
    def possibleStringCount(self, word: str) -> int:
        ans = 1
        for i in range(1, len(word)):
            if word[i] == word[i-1]:
                ans += 1
        return ans
```
This is O(n), O(1).

Let's maybe search memory: There is a LeetCode problem "Find the Original Typed String" (maybe 3370?) where answer is number of groups with length > 1? Wait I recall a solution:
```
class Solution:
    def possibleStringCount(self, word: str) -> int:
        ans = 0
        i = 0
        while i < len(word):
            j = i
            while j < len(word) and word[j] == word[i]:
                j += 1
            ans += j - i - 1
            i = j
        return ans + 1
```
Equivalent. Some solutions maybe:
```
ans = 1
for i in range(1, len(word)):
    if word[i] == word[i-1]:
        ans += 1
return ans
```

"Although Alice tried to focus on her typing, she is aware that she may still have done this at most once." This is exactly LeetCode "Find the Original Typed String" (maybe problem 3364?). The solution is indeed count consecutive equal pairs + 1. Let's recall from memory: There is a LeetCode problem "Find the Original Typed String" with solution:
```
class Solution:
    def possibleStringCount(self, word: str) -> int:
        ans = 1
        for i in range(1, len(word)):
            if word[i] == word[i-1]:
                ans += 1
        return ans
```
I remember seeing this exact code. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def possibleStringCount(self, word: str) -> int:
        ans = 1
        for i in range(1, len(word)):
            if word[i] == word[i - 1]:
                ans += 1
        return ans
```
